# Restored Physics
<page-num>155</page-num>
<header>Restored Physics</header>

...[they] exercise [their powers] and reveal themselves to the senses. 
**Fire**, however, lacks its own perceptible body; it only takes up residence in the body of another. For indeed, a glowing coal is not fire itself, but rather ignited wood; nor is a flame fire, but rather ignited smoke. Finally, like a predator, fire always feeds on the property of others; it lives upon prey, and when that prey is exhausted, the fire is extinguished, because it has nothing within itself from which to be nourished. 

Furthermore, when one physical body is added to another, the total quantity <note>original: *quantitatem*; referring to volume or physical bulk</note> increases; yet fire added to wood or smoke does not do this. Neither the wood nor the smoke is increased in volume by the addition of fire. From this, it is plain to see that it is a **spirit** <term>spirit: *spiritus*; here referring to a subtle, active substance that can penetrate solid matter, distinct from a dense "body"</term> rather than a physical "fiery body" that invades the wood or smoke. 

The fact that a sword can melt while its scabbard remains intact, or that bones can be shattered while the flesh is unharmed by the fiery bolt of a lightning strike <note>original: *fulmen*; the author is referencing the phenomenon of "cold lightning," a common topic in early natural philosophy where lightning was said to affect the hard interior of things while leaving the soft exterior untouched</note>, provides enough evidence that the nature of lightning fire is also spiritual. Nevertheless, one must admit that fire is not entirely immaterial; for [clothed] in matter... 

<meta>catchword: te- (likely leading to "tenuissima" or "very thin/subtle")</meta>

<summary>This page argues that fire is a spiritual substance rather than a physical body, using the examples of how fire consumes fuel without adding volume and how lightning can melt metal without burning its container.</summary>
<keywords>Common Fire, Spirit vs Body, Combustion, Lightning, Vital Heat, Natural Philosophy</keywords>

...[it] consists of the thinnest <note>original: *tenuissima*; completing the word from the previous page</note> substance, through which it clings to the surrounding air, where it can be held back by a thicker substance. Therefore, it has earned the name of **spirit** <term>spirit: *spiritus*; a subtle, active substance rather than a dense, heavy body</term> rather than body, because it is devoid of perceptible quantity <note>referring to physical bulk or volume that can be measured</note> and cannot be detected unless it puts on a foreign body <note>meaning fire only becomes visible when it inhabits a material like wood or wick</note>.

<margin>Light among spiritual things.</margin>
195. Its very origin proves that **Light** must be reckoned among truly spiritual things. Before the forming <term>forming: *informationem*; the process of giving "form" or specific identity to raw matter</term> of primary matter and the birth of the world, there was no light outside of God. However, as Nature was being born, spiritual light began to flow into matter from the fiery Spirit of Divinity, and it began to cling to that matter as if to a **wick** <note>original: *ellichnio*; from the Greek *ellychnion*, the wick of a lamp</note>. From this, the creation and origin of light stood forth. That was the first act of divinity within matter; the first marriage of the creator with the creature, of spirit with body. Therefore, primary and informing light was pure spirit, which by its own fiery power—as if by heat—most intensely rarefied the nearest matter with light...

<page-num>159</page-num>
<header>### Physics Restored</header>

It is characteristic <note>original: *proprium est*; this completes the sentence from the previous page: "For [the nature] of a luminous body is unique..."</note> that it acts through rays, emitting heat and splendor; so that it pours out light far and wide into the world through the shooting forth and multiplication of rays. Let us use the term **light** <note>original: *lux*; the essential, internal power of a luminous source</note> to express simply the primary act of a luminous body; whereas by **radiance** <note>original: *lumen*; the light as it is perceived in the environment</note> we mean the secondary act, which flows from the first.

<margin>198.</margin>
When a wax candle is consumed or extinguished by a sudden wind, the fiery and luminous spirit—which ignites the **wick** <term>wick: *ellychnium*; the part of a candle that holds the flame</term> and the smoke—does not perish or cease to exist as is commonly believed. Instead, being deprived of its fuel <note>original: *pabulo*; literally "food" or "nourishment" for the fire</note> and torn away from it, it is scattered and vanishes into the air. This air is the **abyss** <note>original: *abyssus*; used here to mean a vast, bottomless reservoir</note> and universal receptacle of the lights and spiritual natures of the **material world**. From this we are taught that the nature of light is spiritual and flows from a spiritual source. It is no different from the way natural forms emerge from their matrix, which is the **Spirit of the Universe** <term>Spirit of the Universe: *Spiritus Universi*; the subtle, vital substance believed to permeate all things and link the physical to the spiritual</term>, which flows from the Sun as if from its source... <note>The text breaks off mid-word</note>

<summary>This page distinguishes between "light" (the source) and "radiance" (the effect) and argues that light is a spiritual substance that does not vanish when a flame is extinguished, but rather returns to the air, which acts as a universal reservoir for spiritual natures.</summary>

...perpetually flowing into the mortal world: For just as the bodies of **complex mixtures** <term>complex mixtures: *mixtorum*; physical substances composed of multiple elements</term> flow from **prime matter** <term>prime matter: *materia prima*; the formless, original substance of the universe</term> and the elements at their birth, and gradually sink back into those same principles when they decay; so too do the **natural forms** <note>original: *Formæ naturales*; the internal blueprint or essence that gives an individual thing its specific identity</note> of individual things flow out from the **Universal Form**. This Universal Form, acting as the **Form of Forms**, breathes **formal power** <note>original: *virtutem formalem*; the inherent energy that creates a specific shape or function</note> into the seeds of all things; and when these forms depart, they flow back into it. This Form is indeed the **Spirit of Universal Light**. To this Spirit—as if to their own origin and kindred nature—all solitary sparks of light and forms return once they have been emancipated from their bodily bonds. Thus, all complex things are resolved into their original principles, and those principles themselves are drawn back to the perennial fountain of their nature, as if to their own center or homeland.

<page-num>162</page-num>

### Enchiridion

born for others <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "...and not so much for its own sake as born for others..."</note>, just like a symbol of **divine love** <term>divine love: *divinae charitatis*; in this context, the selfless, expansive love of God that seeks to share itself</term>; sharing itself with all it can reach, it extends even into the most remote places, unless it is blocked by a dense body.

<margin>201.</margin>
**Light** also reveals to us the **infinite nature of God**; for the small flame of a lamp or a candle cannot be exhausted or diminished by the tireless flow of its rays, nor by the infinite sharing of its flame, so long as the fuel <note>original: *pabulo*; literally "fodder" or "food" for the fire</note> lasts. As many rays as there are, just as many streams flow from the flame itself; however much it bestows, and however much is drawn from it, it is neither emptied nor does it suffer any loss. This is a quality that belongs only to a **spiritual nature** and is foreign to a physical nature. In the same way, **intellectual gifts**—such as the sciences and the knowledge of things, which are rightly considered **spiritual lights**—are of this kind: although they are shared very frequently, they always remain whole and complete within their possessor.

<page-num>163</page-num>
<header>Restored Physics <note>original: *Physicæ restitutæ*; this refers to a 17th-century movement to "restore" natural philosophy to a state consistent with both reason and divinity.</note></header>

### It must be admitted that there is something of the divine within light.

<margin>202.</margin>Although the rays of a **luminous body** are of a **spiritual nature**, they are nevertheless halted by a dense body, because they use the air as a **vehicle** <term>vehicle: *vehiculo*; in early science, this referred to a medium that carries a substance or force from one place to another</term>. Without this air, they cannot be perceived by us. Through this connection with the air, the rays themselves also become physical in a certain way; for this reason, they do not penetrate any but **porous bodies**. In the same way, among us, spiritual things act through some sensible medium so that they may be observed in action. However, when the luminous body is absent, the rays also withdraw, and they do not leave its presence, because they flow directly from the body itself.

<margin>203.</margin>It is not only through the presence of the luminous body and its rays, but even when the body is absent and the rays are withdrawn, that the air is permeated with light from a distance by the **luminous spirit** <term>luminous spirit: *spiritu luminoso*; a subtle, active essence thought to be the "soul" or power of light that persists even when the source is obscured</term> coming out from them. This is clearly seen during a total **eclipse of the Sun**, or when the sky is covered by the densest clouds, or even during the night... <note>The sentence continues on the following page.</note>

<sig>L ij</sig>

<summary>This page argues that light, while spiritual in essence, interacts with the physical world through the air and persists as a "luminous spirit" even when its source is obscured, such as during an eclipse.</summary>

shrouded in darkness; as well as when the Sun is submerged below the horizon: for that act of present light—the luminous body and its rays being absent—proceeds from nowhere else than from the approach and presence of the **spiritual light**.

<margin>204. A transparent body.</margin>
A **transparent body** <term>transparent body: *corpus diaphanum*; any substance, like glass or water, that allows light to pass through</term>, such as glass is, when struck by solar rays, gathers them together and receives the image of the Sun into itself. It becomes luminous, becoming a sort of **condensed version of the Sun** <term>condensed version: *compendium*; here meaning a miniature source that contains the power of the original</term>, which emits its own rays into the further part opposite the Sun. From this, it happens that the solar rays, refracted by the meeting with the glass, seem to cross and penetrate the glass itself; however, this does not actually happen in reality. For the rays are stopped around the glass because of the air adhering to them <note>The author believes physical light rays are bound to the air and cannot pass through solid matter; only the "spirit" of the light passes through</note>, with only the **Spirit of light** being transmitted. As for those rays which are poured out on the other side, they are the rays of a "glass Sun"—or rather, they are rays from the glass itself, which has been ignited into a **luminous body** by the solar rays.

<margin>205.</margin> Every **transparent body** <term>transparent body: *diaphanum*; any substance like glass, water, or crystal that allows light to pass through</term>, and especially glass, is a medium for light. This is because it receives light into itself and communicates that received light to the air beyond. This does not happen through the physical movement of "luminous air" from one side to the other—which would be contrary to the laws of Nature—but rather in two other ways. First, because a transparent body is pervious to the **Spirit of light** <note>The author distinguishes between the physical light we see and its "spiritual" or essential force which can penetrate solid matter</note>; the body transmits this Spirit after conceiving it within itself, and once transmitted, the Spirit weaves itself into the air on the other side, resulting in a most abundant light. Second, the transparent medium itself, by the benefit of the light it has received, becomes not merely **illuminated** <note>lit from the outside</note>, but actually **luminous** <note>shining from within</note>. By the Spirit of light, which has an affinity for transparent things, the glass is like a **lamp-wick** <term>lamp-wick: *ellychnium*; the part of a lamp that draws up fuel to keep a flame burning</term> that has been ignited by a flame. Every luminous body possesses the right and the power to pour forth light, a feat which is not permitted to dense and opaque bodies, except by means of **reflection**.

<margin>206.</margin> Even the purely spiritual natures of **mixed bodies** <term>mixed bodies: *mixtorum*; substances created from the combination of the four basic elements (earth, air, fire, water)</term>...

<page-num>166</page-num>
<header>Handbook <note>original: *Enchiridion*</note></header>

[they] are: bodies are their **rinds** and like **earthenware vessels** <note>The author uses the metaphor of *testacea vasa* to describe the physical body as a fragile, protective container for the spirit</note> by which those natures are guarded. Nor could those **sublime natures** <note>Spiritual forces or essential forms</note> have crossed this lower sea and dwelled in this **center of the abyss** <note>A poetic or Hermetic reference to the material Earth, positioned at the lowest point of the celestial hierarchy</note>, unless they were bound to corporeal elements and held down by their weight. The spirits are perceived through bodies, and bodies are moved and act through the spirits; thus they perform mutual services for each other. This is that secret of the **Homeric Juno** <note>In Book 15 of the *Iliad*, Jupiter (Zeus) describes how he punished Juno (Hera) by hanging her from the heavens with two anvils tied to her feet. The author interprets this myth as an allegory for the spirit being weighed down by matter</note>, whom Jupiter sent down with the weight of an anvil attached to her feet.

<margin>Radical moisture is the bond of matter and form.</margin>
223. That **radical ferment** <term>radical ferment: *fermentum radicale*; a life-spark or "leaven" that triggers the transformation of matter</term>, constantly remaining in the depths of mixed bodies, appears to be the bond of the marriage contracted between light and darkness, between **prime matter** <term>prime matter: *materia prima*; the fundamental, formless substance of the universe</term> and **universal form** <term>universal form: *forma vniuersalis*; the spiritual blueprint or essence that gives matter its specific identity</term>; in short, it is a bond between opposites, the seat and the anchor of forms within mixed substances. <margin>The four qualities are the harmonic tones of nature.</margin>
231. The four radical qualities of the **Elements** <term>Elements: the four fundamental building blocks of the material world—fire, air, water, and earth</term> are like the harmonic tones of Nature. They are not contrary to one another, but are distinct and separated from each other by specific intervals. From their rational difference, their **intensification** <note>original: *intensione*; the "stretching" or increasing of a quality</note> and their **lessening** <note>original: *remissione*; the "loosening" or decreasing of a quality</note>, a perfect **harmony of Nature** <note>original: *Naturæ concentus*; a musical concord or "singing together"</note> is created. This harmony is perceived by the intellect, and it is analogous to vocal music, which is perceived by the senses. Deep and high pitches, although they are the extremes in music, are not opposites; rather, they are the boundaries for the middle tones that lie between them, which are composed and tempered in different ratios from those two extremes. In the same way, **heat and cold**, **dryness and moisture** are the extreme qualities in Nature. They are not contrary for that reason, but are the boundaries of the middle and intervening qualities; it is from the mixing and tempering of these extremes that the middle states proceed.

<margin>233.</margin> Nature moves the **machine of the universe** uniformly and in an orderly fashion, yet in such a way that she turns unequal and dissimilar things with unequal and dissimilar motion. The idea that there is a **first mover** <note>original: *primum mobile*; in traditional medieval astronomy, this was the outermost sphere that moved all the inner heavens</note> above the heavens, by whose incredibly rapid daily rotation the lower heavens are turned, is not so much a discovery of **divine wisdom** as it is a crutch for **human ignorance**. <margin>241. Many worlds in the universe.</margin>
Those who think that the almost countless multitude of celestial bodies in the World were created for the benefit of the terrestrial globe and its inhabitants, as if that were their ultimate purpose, are mistaken; <note>original: *hallucinantur*; literally "are hallucinating," meaning they are deluded or holding a false belief</note> for natural reason forbids that far more noble and sublime natures should be enslaved to cheaper and inferior ones. Is it not more likely that each individual globe constitutes its own unique **World**? Is it not more likely that so many Worlds are spread throughout the vast space of the **Ether**, <note>original: *Æther*; in pre-modern physics, this was the "fifth element" believed to fill the space of the universe above the terrestrial sphere</note> like the fiefdoms of a divine and eternal empire? Is it not more probable that they are suspended and bound to one another by that medium as if by a common bond, and that the vastness of the entire universe is made up of those multiple natures? Although these worlds are very distant from one another in both their nature and their location, they nonetheless agree through mutual love, such that they produce a perfect **harmony** in the universe. Heaven, moreover, is the common arena for all things, yet around the more perfect bodies it extends far—

### Physics Restored

...or whether it hangs in the heavens or in its own air: there is doubt regarding its motion; but to what end would the **motion of the Earth** be necessary? Why should it not also stand still among so many fixed bodies? 

And perhaps the **Moon** has its own inhabitants; for it is beyond belief that such immense masses of Orbs should remain idle and sterile, inhabited by no creatures, and that all their movements, actions, and labors should serve only the benefit of this lowest and most vile globe <note>In the traditional Aristotelian view, the Earth was considered the "dregs" of the universe because it was subject to change and decay, unlike the "perfect" heavens.</note>; since **God** himself, impatient with solitude, moving outside of himself through the act of creation, poured himself out entirely into his creatures and commanded them with the law of multiplication <note>original: *multiplicationis legem*; a reference to the biblical command to "be fruitful and multiply."</note>. 

Is it not more consistent with divine goodness and glory to acknowledge that the single fabric of the entire universe is like an empire, adorned with the various natures of many **Worlds**, as if they were provinces or cities? And that these worlds themselves are the dwellings for so many innumerable citizens of different kinds; and that all these things [contribute] to the highest [glory] of the eternal...

...nevertheless, in a certain way, it <note>Referring back to the Divinity mentioned on the previous page.</note> seems to consist of and be perfected by two things: namely, **Intellect** and **Will**. Through the Intellect, God knows all things from eternity; through the Will, He performs all things. Both of these are exercised in the most absolute manner. **Knowledge** <note>original: *Scientia*; here meaning certain, organized knowledge.</note> and **wisdom** pertain to the Intellect; however, **goodness**, **justice**, **mercy**, and the other virtues—which among us are considered moral qualities—belong to the Will. Indeed, even the very **omnipotence** of God is nothing other than His omnipotent Will. 

Intelligible natures <note>A philosophical term for beings possessed of a mind or spirit, capable of understanding abstract truths.</note>—namely, the **Angelic** nature and the **human soul**, which are small reflections <note>original: *imagunculae*; literally "little images."</note> of the Divinity—are also endowed with those same two faculties, according to their own capacity and measure. For in these beings, the Intellect is the instrument <note>original: *organum*; a tool or faculty through which a function is performed.</note> of knowledge, and the Will is the instrument of action; they are capable of nothing beyond these.

# TO THE ANONYMOUS AUTHOR
## To a Philosopher truly Adept. <note>An <term>Adept</term> is a master who has successfully completed the "Great Work" of alchemy, discovering the secret of the Philosophers' Stone.</note>

What the <term>Disciples of Hermes</term> <note>original: *Hermetis Alumni*. These are followers of the Hermetic tradition, named after the mythical figure Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> formerly taught in 
obscure speech, here shines everywhere with 
flashing light.

Ethereal Fountains, from which the 
Moon drinks at a fixed time. 
From this, pregnant with the <term>Salt of Nature</term>, <note>original: *Sale Naturæ*. In alchemy, "Salt" is one of the three prime principles (along with Sulfur and Mercury) representing the body or the stabilizing force.</note> she presses upon her Husband, 
and wrings out the vigorous seed even 
unto death. 
The Woman perishes in the dire death of the Man: 
both turn black: <note>This refers to the *nigredo* or "blackening" phase, the first stage of the alchemical process, signifying decomposition and the stripping away of impurities.</note>

restores life. 
At length, a Child arises more noble than 
its parents, <note>The "Child" represents the Philosophers' Stone, the perfected result of the union of the "king" (Sulfur/Sun) and "queen" (Mercury/Moon).</note>
whom swallowed poisons assist with 
great growth. 
You reveal these great things, Nameless Teacher, 
the glory of the Wise, among whom you 
flourish well-known everywhere.

# HERMETIC

### TO THE STUDENTS, GREETINGS.

AMONG the highest peaks of the <term>Secret Philosophy</term> <note>original: *Arcanæ Philosophiæ*. This refers to the "occult" or hidden knowledge of nature's inner workings, specifically alchemy.</note>, the belief has long prevailed that the work of the <term>Hermetic Stone</term> <note>original: *Hermetici Lapidis*. The "Philosophers' Stone," a legendary substance capable of perfecting both metals and the human body.</note> is the supreme achievement, and something close to a miracle. This belief has grown strong partly because of the mazes of its manifold operations, from which the human mind—unless kindled by a ray of light from a higher source <note>The author suggests that alchemy is not merely a physical craft but a spiritual one requiring divine inspiration or "illumination."</note>—cannot extricate itself. It is also because of the Stone's most noble purpose, which promises a constant abundance of health and of wealth—the two primary summits of a happier life. For this reason, the <term>High Priests</term> <note>original: *Antistites*. A term used to describe the founding masters or authoritative guardians of alchemical knowledge.</note> of this science have, with their tropes... <note>The text ends mid-sentence as the author begins to explain why alchemists used metaphors and symbolic language to hide their secrets.</note>

--- Page 225 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s preface. It explains that the "High Priests" of alchemy used cryptic language to protect their secrets from the unworthy. It also describes the backlash from failed seekers—characterized as greedy "plunderers"—who, unable to achieve the Work, resort to slandering the science as a fraud.</meta>

...with enigmatic discourses, they made it most remote from the knowledge of the common people. They established it on high, like a fortress made most secure by its rocky site, to which access would not lie open unless God Himself showed the way. 

This zeal for concealing the art has brought slander upon the science and its practitioners. For when those unfortunate plunderers of the <term>Golden Fleece</term> <note>original: *velleris aurei*. A mythological reference to the prize sought by Jason and the Argonauts. In alchemical literature, it is a common metaphor for the Philosophers' Stone or the "Great Work."</note>, through their own lack of skill, found themselves thwarted in their vain attempts and realized they were far unequal to such great men, they were seized by a furious desperation. Like <term>Bacchantes</term> <note>original: *bacchantes*. Followers of the wine-god Bacchus known for wild, irrational frenzies; the author uses this to describe the irrational rage of failed alchemists.</note>, they flared up against the reputation of these men and the glory of science, denying that there could be anything real beyond their own limited insight and intellectual power. 

Because they did not apply themselves to honest labor, they have never ceased to accuse the leading masters of the <term>Secret Philosophy</term> <note>original: *arcanæ Philosophiæ*.</note> of falsehood, Nature itself of being powerless, and the Art of being mere trickery. They do this for no other reason than that they rashly condemn what they do not understand; yet even this condemnation does not satisfy their desire for revenge...

<summary>The author explains that alchemical secrets are hidden behind symbolic language to ensure only those guided by God can find them, while warning that failed, greedy seekers often slander the art out of spite.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Canons" or fundamental rules of Hermetic Philosophy. While the previous page focused on the spiritual goals of the art (charity and piety), this page moves into the specific requirements for the practitioner and the definition of the "Stone" itself. It maintains the 17th-century view that alchemy is a divine gift rather than a purely secular craft.</meta>

->### THE SECRET OF HERMETIC PHILOSOPHY ###<-

<margin>2.</margin> The light of this science <note>original: *scientiæ*. In this context, it refers to the sacred knowledge of alchemy.</note> is a gift of God, which He grants through grace to those whom it pleases Him: therefore, let no one turn his mind to its study unless, having first cleansed and purified his heart, and being free from the love and desire for worldly things, he has vowed himself entirely to God.

<margin>3.</margin> The science of making the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapidis Philosophorum*. The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and acting as a universal elixir.</note> is the perfect knowledge of all Nature and Art regarding the kingdom of metals; its practice consists in investigating the principles of metals through analysis <note>original: *analysim*. Here, the process of breaking a substance down into its component parts.</note>, and, after making those parts far more perfect than they were before, joining them together again. The result is a universal medicine <term>medicina catholica<gloss>In the 17th century, "catholic" meant universal or all-encompassing; thus, a medicine that cures all ills.</gloss></term> most powerful for perfecting imperfect metals and for restoring sick bodies, of whatever kind they may be.

<margin>4.</margin> Those who perform public honors and duties, or even those occupied with private and necessary business— <note>The text here ends abruptly due to the page break, following the catchword "occupatio-" below.</note>

<meta>catchword: occupatio- (occupations/business)</meta>

<summary>This page defines the Philosopher's Stone as a "universal medicine" derived from the perfect knowledge of nature and metals, emphasizing that such knowledge is a divine gift reserved for the pure of heart.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Universal Medicine, alchemy, divine grace, purification, metals, Hermetic science</keywords>

--- Page 232 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Canons" or rules of Hermetic Philosophy from page 208. The previous page ended mid-sentence, describing people who are too busy with worldly affairs to pursue the Great Work. This page continues that warning and outlines the spiritual and mental discipline required of a true philosopher, contrasting them with "vulgar" alchemists who focus only on chemical processes.</meta>

->### THE WORK OF PHILOSOPHY ###<-

...who are constantly occupied with tasks, let them not strive for the highest peak of this philosophy; for it requires the whole man. Once found, it possesses him; once possessed, it claims him from every serious and long business, considering all other things foreign and of no value <note>original: *flocci faciens*, literally "valuing at a tuft of wool," a Latin idiom for treating something as worthless.</note>.

<margin>5.</margin> The student of this doctrine should strip his soul of all wicked impulses, especially pride—which is an abomination of Heaven and the gate of Hell. He should frequently devote himself to prayer, practice works of charity, cling little to the things of the world, flee the gatherings of men, and enjoy constant tranquility, so that the **Mind** <note>original: *Mens*. This refers to the "First Matter," the essential starting substance of the Great Work.</note>, the degrees of fire, and the governance <note>original: *regimen*. Nowhere are the hidden causes of things and the secret motions of Nature demonstrated more clearly or more faithfully.

# THE WORK OF PHILOSOPHY #

Let the reader take care, for the philosophers explain their mysteries with deceitful windings and ambiguous speech—indeed, often seemingly contradictory—driven by a zeal for entangling and hiding the truth, rather than corrupting or destroying it. For this reason, their writings abound with ambiguous terms and **homonyms** <note>Words that sound the same but have different meanings; in alchemy, one word might refer to a common chemical, a planet, or a spiritual state.</note>. However, they strive in nothing so sharply as in concealing their own **golden bough**, <note>The "Golden Bough" is a reference to Virgil's *Aeneid*. # THE HERMETIC SECRET #

17. It must be admitted that **metals** cannot be multiplied by the instinct and labor of Nature alone; yet one may certainly affirm that the power of multiplying is hidden in their depths, and that it is manifested by the help of **art**. In this work, nature requires the assistance of art, and both together complete the whole. <note>The alchemical "Art" refers to the skills and processes of the alchemist, which "perfect" what nature has started but left unfinished.</note>

18. He who knows how to extract this by **philosophical solution** <note>This is not a common chemical dissolving, but a way to "open" the metal to reach its inner essence without destroying its "seed" or life-force.</note> has entered the **royal way**, for:

> *are the seeds of gold, although they recede*

19. Others preferred that **Mercury** be added to the Sun; some chose **sulfur** and mercury; others favored the **salt of nature** mixed with those two. <note>These represent the *Tria Prima* or Three Primaries: Sulfur (the soul/flammability), Mercury (the spirit/fluidity), and Salt (the body/stability). Alchemists debated which combination was the true starting point for the Stone.</note>

...assigned no small part in so great a work. These same authors have declared that their **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life.</note> is created from only one thing, sometimes from two, sometimes from three, sometimes from four, and even from five; thus, while writing with such varied speech about the same subject, they nevertheless agree in their underlying intention.

<margin>20.</margin> So that we may act candidly and in good faith, having removed all deceptions <note>original: *sublatis insidiis*. Alchemical authors often admitted to using "snares" or intentional riddles to hide their secrets from the uninitiated.</note>, we maintain that the entire work is completed by only two bodies—namely, the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>Sol and Luna: Alchemical code for philosophical Gold and Silver.</note>—when they are properly prepared. For this is a pure **generation**, which occurs through **Nature** with **Art** serving as its minister, in which the union of male and female takes place, and from which an offspring far more noble than its parents is brought forth. <note>The alchemist acts as a "midwife" to nature, facilitating a "marriage" between the masculine (Gold) and feminine (Silver) principles to produce the Stone.</note>

<margin>21.</margin> Moreover, those bodies must be taken in their pure and uncorrupted virginity; they must be living and animated, not dead and extinguished like the common metals handled by the masses. For who would expect life from the dead? Those are called "corrupted"... <note>The text cuts off here. "Corrupted" likely refers to "vulgar" or commercial metals that have been melted in a forge, which alchemists believed "killed" the metallic life-force or "seed" within them.</note>

<summary>The author clarifies that while older texts use confusing numbers of ingredients, the Great Work requires only two: the "living" principles of Gold and Silver. This process is described as a biological generation or marriage facilitated by the alchemist's art.</summary>

This refers to the process of sublimation, where the substance evaporates and rises within the sealed glass vessel.</note>.

# THE WORK OF PHILOSOPHY #

...at this point, let no one presume to attempt a union against Nature, nor conceive any hope of offspring from such a coupling; instead, he must join **Gabritius** to **Beya** <note>original: *Gabritium Beiæ*. In alchemical allegory, Gabritius represents the masculine, solar principle (the "King" or "Sulfur"), and Beya represents the feminine, lunar principle (the "Queen" or "Mercury"). Their union—often depicted as incestuous because they spring from the same root—is essential for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, brother to sister,

-> He shall join them in a stable marriage, <-
-> and dedicate her as his own; <-

so that he may from there receive the noble **Son of the Sun** <note>The "Son of the Sun" is the Philosopher's Stone itself, the perfected result of the chemical wedding.</note>.

<margin>24.</margin>
<margin>Chapter 62 of the First Testament.</margin>
To this end, they establish **Sulfur** and **Mercury** as the primary matter of the **Stone**. By the name of "Sulfur," they mean the common Sun and Moon; but by "Mercury," they understand the "Moon of the Philosophers" <note>Alchemists distinguished between "common" minerals and "philosophical" versions, which were purified or "living" essences of those minerals.</note>. Thus, with all deception removed, the pious **Lully** <note>original: *Lullius*. Referring to Ramon Lull (c. 1232–1316), a major figure in medieval philosophy and alchemy whose works often focused on the "mercurial" path.</note> advises his friend *not* to seek to work except with Mercury and Moon for silver, and with Mercury and Sun for gold.

<margin>25.</margin>
Therefore, let no one be deceived by adding a third to these two; for Love does not admit a third, and marriage is bounded by the number two. Love sought beyond that is adultery, not matrimony <note>This is a stern warning that the alchemical process requires only two specific primary substances—the "fixed" (masculine) and the "volatile" (feminine)—and that introducing a third foreign substance will corrupt the purity of the reaction.</note>.

### The Secret of Hermetic Science

# The Work of Philosophy

...whose true separation Nature denies to art; for if any permanent liquid is extracted from the **Sun** <note>original: *à Sole*; in alchemical texts, the Sun represents Gold.</note> through the violence of fire or waters—being merely a portion of the liquefied or forcibly dissolved body—it must not be considered a true separation of the **tincture** <note>The transformative "color" or spiritual essence of the metal.</note>. For the tincture follows its own body and is in no way separated from it. This is a deception of the art <note>The "Art" refers to the practice of Alchemy.</note> unknown to the craftsmen themselves.

<margin>32.</margin> But let it be granted that the tincture is separable from its body; even so, it must be admitted that it cannot be separated without the corruption of both the body and the tincture. Since those artists inflict violence upon the **Gold** <note>original: *Auro*</note> through the fire of fusion (the destroyer of Nature) or through **strong waters** <term>Aqua fortis: corrosive mineral acids, such as nitric acid, which were used to dissolve metals.</term>, which gnaw rather than truly dissolve; the body is therefore despoiled of its tincture and its **golden fleece** <note>A mythological metaphor for the precious, life-giving essence of the metal.</note>. It becomes utterly worthless and turns into a useless weight to the ruin of its craftsman, and it is inevitable that the corrupted tincture will perform less effectively.

### The Hermetic Secret

<margin>33.</margin> Finally, let them cast their **tincture** <note>The concentrated spiritual essence or "color" supposed to transform base metals into gold.</note> into **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*; Quicksilver, often considered the "mother" of metals in alchemy.</note>, or into any other imperfect body, and let them bind both together as tightly as the strength of the art allows; yet they will fail in their hope in two ways. # Arcanum Hermeticæ: The Hermetic Secret

...those seeking the prize are carried between the dangerous rocks of the **Sulfur** and **Mercury** of the philosophers. The more insightful, through the constant reading of more serious and trustworthy authors, and with the Sun <note>The "Sun" here refers to both the light of reason and the alchemical symbol for Gold or the active principle.</note> shining light upon them, have gained knowledge of **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur: In alchemical theory, one of the two primary principles; it represents the active, masculine, and combustible nature of matter.</term>; but they remain stuck at the threshold of the **Mercury of the philosophers** <term>Philosophical Mercury: The 'First Matter' in liquid form, representing the feminine, volatile, and universal spirit; not to be confused with common quicksilver.</term>. For writers have diverted it with so many twists and turns, and shrouded it in so many ambiguous names, that it reveals itself to the seeker's mind by a sudden flash of insight rather than through logic or physical toil.

<margin>37.</margin> In order to submerge their **Mercury** in even deeper darkness, the philosophers have made it manifold. In every part and regime <note>original: *regimine*; the specific sequences of heat or operations used to govern the alchemical process.</note> of the work, they have established their own **Mercury**, yet each one is different; nor will anyone attain a perfect knowledge of it who remains ignorant of any part of the work.

41. 45. **T**hat Mercury of the philosophers is not quicksilver in its [raw] nature, nor in its entire substance, but its middle and pure essence which took its origin from it and was created from it. The experience of the more esteemed philosophers supports this opinion.

# The Hermetic Secret #

[...ra]dical moisture of metals; for this reason, it was called **glass** by some of the ancients. For glass is drawn out from a **radical moisture** <term>Radical moisture: In alchemy, this is the fundamental, life-giving fluid or "root" humidity within matter that allows it to be flexible and alive, rather than brittle and dead.</term> stubbornly clinging to ashes, which yields only to the most extreme flame; yet our **central Mercury** reveals itself by the gentlest, albeit longer, **Fire of Nature**.

<margin>49.</margin>
Some have sought the hidden **philosophical Earth** <term>Philosophical Earth: The solid, fixed principle in the alchemical work, representing the "body" that must be purified and reunited with the spirit.</term> through **calcination** <term>Calcination: The process of heating a material to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash," symbolizing the death of the old form.</term>; others through sublimation; some among glazing vessels; others between vitriol and salt, as if between natural vessels. Others instructed to sublimate from lime and glass. 

<margin>Genesis, chapter 1.</margin>

### The Secret of the Hermetic Art ###

That leprosy <note>In alchemical literature, "leprosy" refers to the surface impurities or "accidents" that tarnish a metal, rather than its true inner essence. It is a corruption that can be cured.</note> infesting the body of **Mercury** is not from its root and substance, but is accidental, and therefore separable from it. The **Earthy** impurity is wiped away through a moist bath and the washing of Nature. <margin>*</margin> The **Watery** impurity is put to flight by a dry bath <note>original: "balneum siccum"; a "dry bath" typically refers to heating a substance in a vessel surrounded by sand or ashes rather than liquid, allowing for a gentle, indirect heat.</note> through the gentle fire of generation. Thus, through a triple washing and purging, the **Dragon** <note>The "Dragon" is a powerful alchemical symbol for the volatile matter—raw Mercury—which must be "slain" (fixed) or "stripped" of its impurities to reach a perfected state.</note> is renewed, stripped of its ancient scales and filthy skin.

<margin>51.</margin> The philosophical **sublimation** <term>Sublimation: The process of heating a substance so it turns into vapor and then condenses back into a solid. To alchemists, this was a way to "spiritualize" or purify matter by lifting it away from its heavy, earthy dross.</term> of Mercury is completed in two ways: by removing what is superfluous from it, and by introducing what is lacking. The superfluous things are external accidents, which cloud the glowing **Jupiter** <note>Jupiter represents the metal Tin; in this context, it symbolizes a bright, silvery, and more refined stage of the work.</note> with the dark sphere of **Saturn** <note>Saturn represents Lead and the "blacker than black" stage of the process (the nigredo), associated with heaviness, cold, and impurity.</note>. Therefore, separate the emerging lividness of Saturn until the purple star of Jupiter smiles upon you. Add the **Sulfur of Nature**, of which Mercury indeed possesses enough of a grain and ferment <note>original: "fermentum"; a substance that acts like leaven, allowing the alchemical matter to "grow" or transform other substances.</note> within itself to suffice for its own needs, but see to it that it also suffices for others. Multiply, therefore...

<margin>57.</margin> The philosophers extract their Stone from seven stones, of which two principal ones are of diverse nature and power; one pours in invisible **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur: In the alchemical triad, Sulfur represents the active, masculine, and "combustible" principle associated with heat.</term>, the other spiritual **Mercury** <term>Mercurium: The passive, feminine principle associated with fluidity and "moisture."</term>: the former introduces heat and dryness, while the latter introduces coldness and moisture; thus by their help, the powers of the elements are multiplied in the Stone. The first is found in the eastern region; the latter in the western: both have the power of tinting and multiplying; and unless the Stone has drawn its first **tincture** <note>original: "tincturâ"; the concentrated essence or power of transformation that allows the Stone to "dye" or change the nature of other metals.</note> from them, it will neither tint nor multiply.

64. <margin>Demonstrative means.</margin> Demonstrative means, or signs, are the colors that affect the matter successively and in order, revealing its transformations and states. Three of these are principal ones to be noted, serving as critical indicators, though some authors add a fourth.

The first is **black**, which is called the **Raven’s Head** <note>original: "caput corvi"; a famous alchemical symbol for the *Nigredo* or blackening stage, representing the death and decomposition of the base matter.</note> because of the intense blackness that emerges. At this time, the "grain" putrefies and breaks down so that it may become more fit for generation.

The black color is succeeded by **white**, in which the first degree of completion and the perfection of **White Sulfur** <term>White Sulfur: the refined state of the matter capable of transmuting metals into silver.</term> is achieved. This is called the **Blessed Stone**; this is the **white foliated earth** <note>original: "terra alba foliata"; refers to the purified, flaky, or layered substance that appears during the *Albedo* or whitening stage.</note>, in which the philosophers sow their gold.

The third color is **citrine** <note>original: "citrinus"; a yellowish or lemon-gold color.</note>, which is produced during the transition from the white stage to the red—

<margin>72.</margin> The entire process of the **philosophical work** is nothing other than **dissolution and coagulation** <note>original: "solutio & congelatio"; the fundamental alchemical maxim *solve et coagula*, meaning to break down a substance to its essence and then reform it into a higher state.</note>; the **dissolution of the body** and the **congealing of the spirit**, yet the operation of both is one and the same. As long as these confused natures wander within the spirit, that mixed spirit maintains a middle nature between body and spirit, fixed and volatile.

86. Two things are most to be avoided in the movement of this circle; first, that it be not moved too intensely; second, that it be not moved longer than is right. Accelerated motion stirs up confusion in the **matter** <term>Materia: The raw physical substance being transformed in the alchemical vessel.</term>, so that the thick, impure, and undigested part flies off together with the pure and subtle part, and the undissolved body flies away mixed with the dissolved **spirit** <term>Spiritus: The volatile, active principle of a substance that can be released or "raised" through heat.</term>. By such headlong motion, the celestial and terrestrial natures are confounded, and the spirit of the **quintessence** <term>Quinta essentia: The "fifth essence" or purest part of a substance, beyond the four physical elements, often considered the goal of the work.</term>, corrupted by its mixture with the earth, becomes dull and weak. Through excessively prolonged movement, the earth is too greatly emptied of its spirit, and is rendered so languid, dry, and destitute of spirit that it cannot easily be restored or recalled to its proper balance <note>original: "temperamentum"</note>. Either error burns the **tinctures** <term>Tinctura: The powerful, transformative essence capable of "tinging" or changing the properties of other substances.</term>, or even turns them to flight <note>In alchemical terms, "turning to flight" refers to the substance evaporating or escaping the vessel due to excessive heat.</note>.

<margin>
The second
circle.

The name **Fire Against Nature** <note>original: "Ignis contra naturam." This refers to the third type of alchemical fire mentioned on the previous page, typically associated with the dissolution of the matter.</note> has been given to it because, in a certain sense, it acts according to Nature; for while the **specific form** <note>In alchemical philosophy, the "form" is the essential identity of a substance. Here, the author argues that while the outer appearance changes, the inner essence is preserved through the process.</note> is saved, it breaks down the matter in such a way that it prepares it for **generation** <note>original: "generationem." <margin>95.</margin>

This is the internal heat or life-force already present within the metal or matter being worked upon.</note>, but is rather its first stage. For the order of Nature requires that **corruption** <note>The process of "Nigredo" or blackening, where the substance is dissolved and "dies" before it can be reborn.</note> must precede generation. Thus, the innate fire, obeying the law of Nature, carries out both tasks by successively stirring a double movement in the matter. The first is the slower movement of corruption, brought about by a weak heat to soften and prepare the body. The second is the more vigorous movement of generation, driven by a more intense heat to bring life to and fully **inform** <note>original: "informandum." To give a substance its final, perfected "form" or nature.</note> the **elementary body** <note>The physical substance composed of the elements, now ready to receive its new spiritual or perfected form.</note> that has already been prepared by the first stage. Therefore, a double motion is produced from two degrees of heat belonging to the same Fire, nor [is] the Fire...

...me <note>original: "flammæ," completing the word "flam-" from the previous page. The sentence refers to the fire or flame mentioned previously.</note> must be moved by degrees <note>original: "per puncta," literally "by points."</note>, whether it is increased or decreased, so that, following the example of Nature, the matter may proceed little by little and of its own accord toward its formation <note>original: "informationem." In alchemical philosophy, this is the process by which matter receives its final "form" or essential nature.</note> and completion; for nothing is so foreign to Nature as violence. Therefore, the **Philosopher** <note>term: Philosopher. Here meaning a practitioner of the "Hermetic Philosophy" or alchemy.</note> should set before himself for consideration the slow approach and retreat of the **Sun**, whose torch and lamp grant its heat to the things of the **World** and impart a tempered balance, according to the reckoning of the seasons and the laws of the **Universe**.

<margin>
The points 
of 
Fire.
</margin>
**104.** The first point of the heat of the **bath** is called "feverish heat" or the "heat of dung" <note>original: "fimi." Alchemists often used the heat generated by fermenting horse manure to provide a very gentle, constant warmth for digestion.</note>; the second is simply the heat of the bath. The first point of the second degree is the simple heat of ashes; the second, however, is the heat of sand. The points of the fire of coals and of flame lack a specific name, but are distinguished by their intensity and reduction through the operation of the **Intellect** <note>original: "Intellectus." This implies that at higher temperatures, where specific mediums like water or sand cannot be used to regulate heat, the alchemist must rely on their own observation and mental judgment.</note>.

<summary>This page explains that alchemical fire must be regulated gradually to mimic the natural cycles of the sun, avoiding "violence" to the matter. It categorizes specific heat levels, ranging from the gentle warmth of dung and water baths to the intense, unmeasured heat of coals and open flames.</summary>

Sometimes only three degrees of fire are named by the **philosophers** <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers specifically to alchemists or practitioners of the Hermetic arts.</note>, namely those of the **bath**, of **ashes**, and the **burning fire**, which includes the fire of coals and of flame. In this way, authors generally shroud the light of the **Philosophers' Fire** <note>term: Philosophers' Fire. A metaphorical and physical heat required for alchemical transformation, often kept secret by authors to prevent the uninitiated from succeeding.</note> in darkness through varied ways of speaking; for the knowledge of this fire is held among their most important secrets.

Through the first degree, the **eclipse** of the **Sun** and **Moon** <note>This refers to the *nigredo* or blackening stage, where the matter "dies" and turns black.</note> occurs; through the second, the light of the **Moon** begins to be restored; through the third, the **Moon** attains the fullness of its splendor; however, through the fourth, the **Sun** is exalted to the highest peak of its glory. In any stage, indeed...

...part, the **Fire** is administered according to the rule of **geometry**, so that the active agent responds to the disposition of the "patient" <note>In alchemy, the "agent" is the active force (fire or catalyst) and the "patient" is the matter being acted upon.</note>, and their strengths are balanced against each other on a fair scale.

107. The **philosophers** have devoted themselves greatly to the desire of hiding their **Fire**, so much so that they have scarcely dared to touch upon it directly. Instead, they indicate it through the description of qualities and properties rather than by name; such as calling it a fire that is aerial, vaporous, moist and dry, clear, or astral, because it can easily be increased or decreased step by step at the will of the craftsman. For those desiring more knowledge of Fire, the works of **Lully** <note>Refers to Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1316), a philosopher and polymath to whom many influential alchemical treatises were attributed.</note> will satisfy them, for he openly revealed the secrets of practice to sincere souls <note>original: "candidis animis," literally "bright" or "white" souls, meaning those with pure intentions.</note>.

108. Various things have been written about the conflict of the **Eagle** and the **Lion** <note>term: Eagle and Lion. ...up to ten: The fewer there are, the heavier the struggle and the slower the victory; however, the more **Eagles** <note>In this context, the "Eagles" represent the volatile agents (often mercury) used to break down the "Lion" or solid matter.</note> there are, the shorter the fight and the swifter the tearing apart of the **Lion** will follow. A more auspicious number of seven **Eagles** may be taken from the teachings of **Lully** <note>Ramon Llull, a major figure in alchemical literature.</note>, or nine from **Senior** <note>Senior Zadith, an influential Arabic alchemist whose works were translated into Latin.</note>.

<margin>109. Vessels of Nature and art.</margin>
The **vessel** in which the philosophers cook down their work is of two kinds: one of **Nature**, and the other of **art**. The vessel of **Nature**, which is also called the **vessel of philosophy**, is the "earth" of the **stone**, or the female, or even the **matrix** <note>The "matrix" or womb represents the receptive material that allows the "male" seed to transform.</note>, in which the seed of the male is received, putrefies <note>original: "putrefit." In alchemy, putrefaction was the essential stage of decay that preceded the birth of something new and perfected.</note>, and is prepared for generation. However, the vessel of **art** is threefold; for the **secret** <note>original: "arcanum," the hidden essence or secret substance being sought.</note> is cooked in a threefold vessel.

<margin>110.</margin>
The first vessel of **art** is made from a transparent stone, or from **stony glass** <note>Refers to high-quality, heat-resistant glass or crystal used in laboratory apparatus.</note>; some of the philosophers have hidden its form with a certain enigmatic description; at one time [describing it] as being from three, a third, from two...

...pieces, namely the **alembic** <note>The head or cap of a distilling apparatus, used to collect vapors.</note> and the **cucurbit** <note>The lower, gourd-shaped vessel containing the substance to be heated; also known as a gourd-glass.</note>, asserting it is composed of the three aforementioned parts, with the addition of a cover.

<margin>111.</margin>
Most authors have invented a multiplicity of such **vessels** as necessary for the **philosophical work** <note>The "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of transforming base matter into the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, calling them by various names according to the diversity of the operations, with the intention of hiding the **secret** <note>original: "arcani." Alchemists often used "Decknamen" (cover-names) to protect their secrets from the unworthy.</note>. For they spoke of a dissolving vessel for **solution**, a putrefying vessel for **putrefaction**, a distilling vessel for **distillation**, a subliming vessel for **sublimation**, a calcining vessel for **calcination**, and other similar things.

<margin>112.</margin>
But so that we may act honestly, with all deception removed, a single **vessel of Art** is sufficient for finishing the work of both **Sulfurs** <note>In alchemy, "Sulfur" often represents the active, fixed, and masculine principle of the Stone. The "two Sulfurs" likely refer to the white and red stages or the internal and external agents.</note>; for the work of the **Elixir** <note>The final, perfected substance capable of transmutation and healing.</note>, another is used. For the diversity of the **digestions** <note>Prolonged heating at a gentle, steady temperature.</note> does not require a change of vessels; indeed, one must take the greatest care that the vessel is not changed or opened until the end of the first work.

<summary>The author explains that while traditional alchemists used many specialized names for vessels to confuse outsiders, the actual Hermetic process requires only one vessel for the primary stages to avoid disrupting the chemical "digestion."</summary>

<margin>113.</margin>For the shape of the **glass vessel**, you shall choose one that is round or even oval at the bottom—that is, in the **cucurbit** <term>cucurbita: the lower, gourd-shaped part of a distilling apparatus</term>—with a neck extending at least a palm’s width or more. It should be sufficiently wide but with a narrow mouth, made in the manner of a flask, seamless and continuous. It must be thick everywhere so that it may withstand a long and sometimes intense fire. This cucurbit is called "blind" <note>original: "cæca."</note> because its "eye" is blinded by the **Hermetic seal** <note>original: "sigillo Hermetico." An airtight seal, often created by melting the glass neck of the vessel shut to prevent the escape of vapors.</note>, so that no foreign matter may enter, and the **spirit** <note>Refers to the volatile components of the alchemical mixture that would evaporate if not sealed.</note> cannot withdraw itself.

<margin>114.</margin>The second vessel of the art is made of wood, from an oak trunk cut into two hollow hemispheres. In this, the **philosophers' egg** <note>original: "ouum philosophorum." A symbolic name for the sealed glass vessel where the "embryo" of the Stone is incubated.</note> is nurtured until it "hatches" <note>original: "pullificet." This describes the stage where the internal matter begins to transform and "bring forth" the new substance.</note>; regarding this, see the "Fountain" of **Trevisan** <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a celebrated alchemical author known for his allegorical descriptions of the process.</note>.

<margin>115.</margin>The practitioners called the third vessel their **furnace** <note>original: "furnum."</note>, which guards the other vessels along with the material and the entire work. The philosophers also made a point of hiding this among their secrets.

The furnace, the guardian of the secrets, is called the **Athanor** <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a steady, prolonged heat, vital for alchemical "digestion."</term> because of the "immortal" fire <note>original: "ab igne immortali." Alchemists often linked the name Athanor to the Greek word *athanatos* (deathless), as the furnace was designed to stay lit for weeks or months at a time.</note> which it perpetually guards; for it provides a continuous—though sometimes varying—fire to the work, which reason dictates must be administered as greater or lesser according to the quantity of the **matter** <note>The chemical or symbolic substances being processed.</note> and the capacity of the furnace.

<margin>117.</margin>Let the material of the furnace be of baked brick, or even of rich earth, or clay most thoroughly ground and prepared with horse dung and mixed hair <note>Dung and hair were standard "refractory" additives used to reinforce the clay and prevent the furnace walls from cracking under the stress of constant heat.</note>, so that it may adhere more tenaciously and not become full of cracks from long-lasting heat. <margin>118.</margin>Let the shape of the furnace be round, with an interior height of two feet or thereabouts; in the middle of this, a round iron or brass plate should be placed, having the thickness of the back of a knife, occupying almost the entire interior width of the fur- <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the description of the furnace's internal structure on the following page.</note>

# The Secret of the Hermetic Art

<page-num>284</page-num>

...[the Elixir borrows its] **fixation** from the **Ferment** <term>Fixation<note>In alchemy, this is the process of making a volatile substance "stable" or solid so that it can withstand the heat of a furnace without evaporating.</note></term>; its **penetration** from the **Sulfur** <term>Penetration<note>The power of the Elixir to seep into the innermost parts of a base metal to transform its nature.</note></term>; and its **fusion** from **Quicksilver** <note>original: "Argento viuo"</note>, which is the medium for joining the **tinctures**—namely, those of the **Ferment** and the **Sulfur**. Furthermore, it borrows its **multiplicative power** infused from the **Spirit of the Quintessence** <term>Quintessence<note>The "fifth element," a celestial and incorruptible force believed to be hidden within all matter; it provides the life-force that allows the Stone to grow in power.</note></term>.

126. Two perfect metals exhibit a perfect **tincture** <note>A "tincture" is the transformative color or quality of the Stone that "dyes" or transmutes base metals into gold or silver.</note>, because they have been dyed by the pure **Sulfur** of nature; therefore, no metallic **Ferment** should be sought outside of these two bodies. Dye your white and red **Elixir**, then, with the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>The "Sun" (Sol) and "Moon" (Luna) are the standard alchemical names for Gold and Silver.</note>; **Mercury** is the first to receive their tincture, and once received, it communicates it to others.

127. In composing the **Elixir**, take care that you do not change or mix the **Ferments**, for each **Elixir** enjoys its own specific **Ferment** and desires its own specific elements. <margin>128.</margin> The **second work** is cooked in the same or a similar vessel, in the same furnace, and with the same degrees of heat as the first, but it is completed in a shorter amount of time.

<margin>129. The three humors of the stone.</margin> There are three **humors** <term>Humors<note>In ancient and alchemical science, 'humors' refers to the vital fluids or moistures within a substance. They were believed to carry the life-force and specific elemental qualities of the matter.</note></term> in the **Stone**, which must be extracted one after another: namely, the **watery**, the **airy**, and the **radical**. Therefore, all the labor and care of the practitioner centers upon the humor; no element other than the **moist** is circulated in the work of the **Stone**. For the **Earth** <note>In this context, 'Earth' refers to the solid, base material or 'body' of the substance.</note> must, before all else, be dissolved and liquefied within the humor. Of all these, however, the **radical humor**—the very residence of **Fire** <note>original: "Ignis habitus." The radical humor was thought to be the "fuel" of the innate heat of a substance; if it is destroyed, the substance dies.</note>—is the most tenacious, because it is bound to the center of Nature, from which it is not easily separated. Therefore, extract those three humors gradually and successively through their own "wheels" by **dissolving** and **congealing** <term>Dissolving and congealing<note>Based on the famous alchemical motto "Solve et Coagula," this refers to the repetitive cycle of breaking a substance down into liquid and then solidifying it again to purify it.</note></term>; for by the manifold and alternating repetition of **Dissolution** and **Congelation**, the "wheel" is extended, and the entire work is completed.

<summary>This page outlines the practical requirements for the 'second work' of alchemy and explains the three essential moistures (humors) that must be extracted and refined. It emphasizes that the entire process relies on the cycle of dissolving and solidifying these fluids to reach the 'radical humor' at the center of nature.</summary>

<margin>130.</margin> The perfection of the **Elixir** <term>Elixir<note>The final, perfected substance of the alchemical Great Work, believed to possess the power of transmutation and healing.</note></term> consists in the tight union of the **dry** and the **moist**, and in an **indissoluble marriage** <note>original: "indissolubili matrimonio." Alchemists often used the metaphor of marriage to describe the chemical bonding of substances that cannot be separated once joined.</note>, so that they may never be separated; rather, the dry should flow into the moist with a moderate heat, remaining stable even under the full pressure of the fire. The sign of perfection is if a tiny bit of it, cast upon a glowing-hot iron or copper plate, flows immediately without any smoke.

<margin>131.</margin> Take three weights of **Red Earth** or **Red Ferment** <term>Ferment<note>A substance added to the alchemical base to "leaven" it, directing the transformation toward a specific quality—in this case, the "Red" or solar/gold state.</note></term>, and two weights of **Water** and **Air** combined; let them be ground together and mixed thoroughly. Let an **amalgam** <note>original: "amalgama." A soft, pliable blend of substances.</note> be made, like butter or a metallic paste, so that the softened **Earth** is imperceptible to the touch. Add one and a half weights of **Fire** <note>In this context, "Fire" likely refers to a specific sulfurous or "hot" substance rather than literal flame.</note>. Let these be digested in their own vessel, most tightly sealed, with a **Fire of the first degree** <note>The lowest level of heat, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen or a compost heap.</note>, as much as may suffice; then, let the elements be extracted in their proper order by their own degrees of fire. These, having been turned downward with a slow motion, should be **fixed** in their own **Earth**, so that no volatile parts may escape from there...

<margin>* Multiplication of the Elixir.</margin>
Since in the **Elixir**, the powers of the **Fire of nature** have been most abundantly multiplied through the inspired Spirit of the <term>quintessence<note>The "fifth essence" or purest form of a substance, beyond the four physical elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note></term>, and the corrupt qualities of bodies—which besieged their purity and the true light of Nature with darkness—have been erased through long and manifold <term>sublimations<note>The process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used to purify material.</note></term> and <term>digestions<note>A slow heating process where a substance is kept at a constant temperature to mature or refine it.</note></term>, therefore the **Fiery Nature**, freed from its shackles and strengthened by the help of celestial forces, acts most powerfully when enclosed in this, our **fifth Element**. # HERMETIC
## PHILOSOPHY
### TO THE LOVERS [GREETINGS] <note>original: "Amatoribus. S." The 'S' stands for *Salutem* (Greetings). "Lovers" refers to the "Lovers of the Art," a standard term for students of alchemy.</note>
### I.C. CHYMIERASTES <note>A pseudonym combining the Greek words *chymia* (alchemy/chemistry) and *erastes* (lover), meaning "Lover of Alchemy."</note>

**E**ven such is the difference between the **living** philosophy of the Hermeticists and the **dead** philosophy of the Pagans <note>original: "Ethnicorum." A vital alchemical concept referring to the inner wisdom or divine spark within the natural world that guides the chemist.</note> into the souls of his own, by whose benefit all the shadows of error are immediately and utterly driven away and expelled from there; this latter, however, its own...

# The Hermetic Secret

...but rather that you might acknowledge it was won by your own labors. In contrast, cast your eyes upon the beginning of the truly **Golden Treatise** <note>original: "Tractatus verè aurei." This refers to the *Septem Capitula* or "Seven Chapters," a foundational alchemical text attributed to Hermes Trismegistus.</note> of seven Chapters by your Father **Hermes** concerning the secret of the <term>Physical Stone</term> <note>original: "Lapidis Physici." More commonly known as the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting eternal life.</note>, and observe how piously he thinks concerning God, the giver of this secret Knowledge: for **Hermes** says:

> In so long a lifetime, I have not ceased to experiment, nor have I spared my soul from labor; I obtained this art and science solely by the inspiration of the living God, who deigned to reveal it to me, His servant. Indeed, He has granted the power of judgment to those who are rational to decide, but He has left no occasion for sinning to anyone. As for me, if I did not fear the Day of Judgment, or the damnation of my soul for the concealment of this knowledge, I would reveal nothing of this science, nor would I prophesy <note>original: "prophetisarem." The author views the transmission of alchemical secrets as a form of prophetic duty.</note> it to anyone. But I wished to pay my debt to the faithful, just as the **Author of Faith** <note>original: "Author fidei." A title for God, emphasizing Him as the source of all belief and truth.</note> has deigned to bestow it upon me.

### The Work of Philosophy

...mes <note>original: "mes." This syllable completes the name "Hermes" from the final line of the previous page.</note>, than which, in my opinion, nothing wiser nor more suitable for the **Christian Religion** could ever have been put forward. And it is for this reason that all men of more sublime genius and judgment, as many as there are and have been, have embraced with their whole heart, soul, and strength the living, sacred, and divine **Philosophy** of **Hermes** himself—rejecting that dead, profane, and merely human philosophy of the <term>Pagans</term> <note>original: "Ethnicorum." Here referring to secular or worldly philosophers, such as the un-baptized Aristotle, whose work the author views as "dead" compared to the "living" Hermetic tradition.</note>—and have commended and illuminated it in their various writings and scholarly works.

From all of these, to confess frankly, since it has been my lot until this day to read no writer more true, more concise, or more clear than the anonymous author of these two **Treatises**—who is indeed anonymous, but truly an **Adept Philosopher** <note>original: "Philosopho verè Adepto." # Bibliographic Notes on Alchemical Treatises

by Eirenæus Philoponus Philalethes <note>A pseudonym meaning "Peaceful Lover of Labor and Truth." This name was used by George Starkey (1628–1665), a Harvard-educated chemist who moved to London and became a central figure in the alchemical circles that included Robert Boyle.</note>
The Marrow of Alchemy; <note>A significant English alchemical poem published in two parts in 1654 and 1655, intended to explain the secrets of the "philosopher's stone."</note>
printed in the year <note>original: "an^o"; abbreviation for the Latin "anno."</note> 1654 at the Sign of the Crown in St. Paul’s Churchyard. <note>St. Paul’s Churchyard was the traditional center of the London book trade; the "Sign of the Crown" identifies the specific shop of the bookseller Giles Calvert.</note>
*Euphrates* or *Waters of the East* <note>A mystical and alchemical work published in 1655 by Thomas Vaughan, writing under the name Eugenius Philalethes.</note>
[Published by] Moseley <note>Humphrey Moseley was one of the most prominent London publishers of the mid-17th century, known for his catalogs of literary and philosophical works.</note> by Philalethes
